AGENTS.md names this as an exception to the rule against deleting inside a bench.
- A snapshot of an encrypted backup is refused and the backup stays in `private/backups`: `site restore` takes plain sets only, so a snapshot that cannot be rolled back to is worse than none.
- `site snapshots` is read only and takes no lock, like `site backups`. `site snapshot` and `site rollback` take `--background` like `site backup` and `site restore`.
- Dropping one snapshot by name refuses the undo point of the last rollback (`.last-rollback`), and `--force-undo-point` is the person's word that it can go: the rollback it undoes is otherwise left with no way back, and `--prune` alone protected it before. The flag is refused for an agent like the drop itself.
- A snapshot checks the free space before `bench backup` runs, as the set copy of `site restore` does: need is the public and private files (`du`), the largest database dump the site has (a snapshot or a backup: the dump is what is written, compressed) and 5 percent, at least 1 MB, against `df` of `sites/SITE`. It is a guess, kept on the safe side, and a `df` that says nothing lets the run go. The rollback checks it in its plan (an error, so it cannot be applied) and again in its snapshot step, because it writes one more set before it replaces anything.
- `site drop` and `bench remove` name the snapshots in their plans (a `snapshots` key only when there are some, so the shape of a site without them is unchanged) and in the message after the archive: `bench drop-site` moves the whole site folder, snapshots included, so a site with snapshots is not a site with only a backup.
- `--to latest` skips a folder that has no manifest or no database dump (a cut short snapshot) and says which it skipped, and a snapshot is made only when the manifest was written: if that fails the set goes back to `private/backups`, the empty folder is removed with `rmdir`, and a folder that cannot be emptied is renamed `.incomplete-ID`, never deleted. The site_config part of the set is checked like the other three.
- Part 1 is the CLI and the MCP server. The Mac app and the tray follow in their own pull requests and read the same JSON.
- A rollback to a snapshot is gated by a lease like a restore (the site), and `site snapshot drop` like `site drop`; taking a snapshot is not gated: it only adds one, like a backup. benchbar_site_rollback takes a `lease` argument.
- Undo points (second review of #277): `.last-rollback` names the `before rollback` snapshot of the last rollback that finished (its verify passed), and moves only then. The snapshot a rollback takes first is appended to `.unfinished-rollbacks` at once, and leaves it only when that same rollback finishes or a person drops the snapshot. Protected (not in `over_limit`, not removed by `--prune`, dropped by name only with `--force-undo-point`) is the last finished undo point plus every unfinished one. Rollback A ok, B fails halfway, B retried ok: the retry's snapshot is of the half restored site and becomes the undo point; B's first snapshot holds the real data from before B and stays protected; A's snapshot is released. A failed run never moves the undo point, so a retry cannot unprotect the data the failure left. The plan warns when an earlier rollback did not finish. An unfinished entry stays until a person drops that snapshot: failures are rare and each one holds data nobody else has.
- A snapshot is built in `.incomplete-ID` and renamed to its name last, so a run cut short at any point leaves nothing a list shows or a rollback reads; a folder that cannot be emptied stays under that hidden name. `site rollback` refuses any snapshot that lacks the manifest, the database dump, the public files, the private files or site_config, however it was named, before a token is given (`site snapshot drop` still takes it by name, for a person to clean up).
- The space check of a snapshot counts the database dump as the largest earlier dump of the site, else the database size from MariaDB (data and index, with the root password the CLI itself uses: in hand, the environment, the Keychain or the 0600 file; never printed), else the size of the files, at least 64 MB. A rollback adds the size of the snapshot it puts back. `df -Pk` (POSIX output, one line per volume).
- `site snapshot drop` is scoped for the lease by its first non option word after `drop` (`fl_lease_snapdrop_site`), so `drop --prune SITE` is gated as `drop SITE --prune` is.
- The Features page is checked against the code (0.9.0, #170): `scripts/docs-reference.py --check` fails when a top level command of `commands --json` or a tool of `mcp.py` is named in no table row of `docs/features.md`, or when one table has the same feature twice. The page stays hand written, because a row needs a platform, a version and a guide that the code does not hold. A command counts as named when a code span of a row starts with its word, so a mention in the prose is not enough. `FEATURES_SKIP` in the script lists the words that need no row, each with a reason; today that is `help`. In `docs/whats-new.md`, "(coming)" is allowed only on a version later than the last release: `FL_VERSION` when it is final, the newest dated section of `CHANGELOG.md` while it is a candidate.

## 0.9.0: CLI structure, part 1 (issue 151)

- `fl_version_lt` is defined once, in `ui.sh` (CORE-16): it is the only library the entry point, the lean start, the phase scripts and every test load, and `version-policy.sh` is not part of the lean start. It is the prerelease aware one (`0.9.0-rc.1` is older than `0.9.0`); `fl_dep_fits` and `fl_notices_since_lt` cut the suffix off first, so a dependency spec still compares numbers only.
- `BENCHBAR_LABS` is a test seam, read only under `FL_TESTING=1` (CORE-21), and has no row in `commands --json`. `scripts/docs-reference.py` sets `FL_TESTING=1` to list every lab's commands. The Mac app still passes the variable through and the Windows shim still forwards it into WSL; both are harmless now and are left for a change in those folders.
- Hand off to an older app CLI (CORE-24): the hand off stays, as the Second CLI check decided (every copy runs the app's CLI, so one version works on the state, and the app is what is behind). The person is told in `--version` (a `via` line after the first line, which the app reads alone) and in `where`. No version test in `install-kind.sh`: a newer standalone CLI running next to the app's older one would write state the app's CLI then reads.
- The helper block follows `$SHELL` (CORE-15): zsh and bash share `shell-helpers.tmpl`, fish has `shell-helpers.fish.tmpl` in `conf.d/benchbar.fish` (a file of our own, so `config.fish` is never edited; the same marker block, so status, replace and removal are the one code path). The fish functions run `command $BENCHBAR`, which every fish version accepts where a bare variable as a command needs fish 3. nushell, xonsh, elvish, tcsh, csh and PowerShell get no file (`fl_rc_file` prints nothing and doctor's helpers check is ok with the long forms named): their syntax and config paths differ by version and no test here can run them. An unknown `SHELL` keeps the platform default.
- bash on macOS: the block goes to the first of `.bash_profile`, `.bash_login`, `.profile` that exists (bash reads only that one), to `~/.bashrc` when that file mentions `bashrc`, and to a new `.bash_profile` only when none exists. A block an older version wrote to another file stays there and `repair` says so: it is valid for the shell that reads it, and removing it is the person's call.
- A read only rc file is refused, not asked about: the rename would replace it whatever its mode, and `repair` also runs without a terminal. A symlink chain stops at 20 links with a refusal.
- Old names (CORE-19): only the two temp file prefixes were renamed. Everything else is either on disk in existing installs (and stays readable until 1.0.0 at the earliest) or the internal `fl_` prefix and `lib/frappe-local/` folder, which CONTRIBUTING.md now names as kept on purpose.
